## 2026-10-13 T-V1-080 — delete fence outranks originals-missing: a tombstoned document discloses DOCUMENT_DELETED, rot stays INGESTION_ERROR

TASK: T-V1-080 (solo, ~25 min box, no subagents). The post-run red T-036's acceptance caught a disclosure regression **my own tickets created**: T-055/T-072 purge a tombstoned document's original, so a job redelivering after a delete hit `OriginalMissing` at the load and disclosed `INGESTION_ERROR` (~`ingest_pipeline.py:633`) *before* the pre-write fence could emit `DOCUMENT_DELETED` (`:102`, raised `:154`). Safety was never at stake (failed, drained, no resurrection — the crash row itself reports `drained=True`, `verdict='PASS'`); only the disclosed code was wrong, and the acceptance record explicitly did not waive it.

CHANGE (my delta = 3 paths, 0 foreign paths touched):
- `apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py` (M — allowed, **fence/load ordering only**) — shape chosen: **fence-first ordering, scoped to the delete case**.
  - `_refuse_tombstoned_document_before_load(session, *, job, version)` — reads `Document.deleted_at` and, when the document is tombstoned, delegates to the **existing** `assert_generation_is_publishable(..., gate="load")`. Delegating rather than re-raising means the compensation, the non-retryable code and the disclosure text are the gate's own, not a second copy that could drift from it. The tombstone is read **fresh from the row**, never from the identity map's dequeue-time copy, for the reason `generation_publication_state` documents.
  - Two call sites in `process_job`, both around the load: **before** `load_original_bytes(version)`, and — for a tombstone that lands *while* the object store is being read — again on an `INGESTION_ERROR` failure before that failure is allowed to stand. Without the second one the fence would still lose the race occasionally, and the ticket's own guarantee ("delete fence wins") has to hold for the code that is disclosed, not just for the happy ordering.
- `tests/integration/test_delete_fence_precedence.py` (?? NEW — 2 tests, one per direction, on the T-071 scratch-collection seam).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: "the object is missing" and "the operator deleted this document" are different events and an operator reads them differently — one is storage rot to investigate, the other is expected and needs no action. Collapsing them trains the reader to ignore `INGESTION_ERROR`. Precedence also has to be resolved somewhere honest, and the tombstone is the only authority that can say which of the two happened; Postgres already holds it.

REUSED: nothing new. The fence machinery is entirely T-019's — `generation_publication_state`, `assert_generation_is_publishable`, `_unpublish_generation`, `_FENCE_MESSAGES`, `Document.deleted_at`; the test seam is T-071's `vector_collection_env.use_scratch_collection` / `assert_not_live` plus T-039's `object_store_env` and `storage.originals` for the rot fixture. No new dependency, config, collection, schema or service.

BUILT: one helper, two call sites, two tests. Deliberately NOT built: a general fence-first for **all** fence reasons. A superseded generation keeps today's ordering, so its disclosed code does not change under a ticket about delete precedence; widening it would have changed a code T-036 does not flag. The alternative shape museR offered (map `OriginalMissing` → `DOCUMENT_DELETED` when tombstoned) was rejected as the primary route because it needs a session inside the load helper and still loses the ordering — the store is read at all for a job that can never publish — but the fence-first route subsumes it for the tombstone case.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `be2bb88` at the end; nothing committed by me):
- **Red reproduced first, unmodified code**: `pytest "tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py::test_redelivery_after_delete_is_fenced_not_resurrected"` → `AssertionError: assert 'INGESTION_ERROR' == 'DOCUMENT_DELETED'`, row `drained=True`, `verdict='PASS'` — the safety half was already green, exactly as museR described.
- **The metric, now green**: `pytest tests/integration/test_delete_fence_precedence.py "…::test_redelivery_after_delete_is_fenced_not_resurrected"` → **3 passed**. Direction 1 asserts `DOCUMENT_DELETED`, **and** `!= "INGESTION_ERROR"`. Direction 2 (`test_missing_original_on_a_live_document_still_discloses_ingestion_error`) uploads through the real path, deletes the object out of the object store, and asserts `INGESTION_ERROR`, **and** `!= "DOCUMENT_DELETED"`. Both assert their **precondition** rather than assuming it: direction 1 proves the tombstone is set *and* the original really is `missing` (so the test cannot pass without exercising the fence/load conflict), direction 2 proves the document is live *and* the object is gone.
- **Both directions proven discriminating by mutation** (a test that passes either way proves nothing):
  - **Mutation A** — fence-first disabled (pre-T-080 ordering): direction 1 fails with `{'error_code': 'INGESTION_ERROR', 'error_message': 'original object is missing from object storage'}`, i.e. the exact original red; direction 2 still passes. **1 failed, 1 passed**.
  - **Mutation B** — `OriginalMissing` collapses to `DOCUMENT_DELETED`: direction 2 fails with "storage rot on a live document must stay INGESTION_ERROR"; direction 1 still passes. **1 failed, 1 passed**.
  - The fixed file was restored from a backup afterwards and re-linted (`ruff` + `mypy` clean), so neither mutation is in the tree.
- **Suites** (run separately, for the reason in RISKS 3): `pytest tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` → **8 passed**; `pytest tests/integration/test_ingestion_jobs.py` → **8 passed**; `pytest tests/integration/test_delete_fence_precedence.py tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` → **10 passed**; `pytest tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py` → **18 passed** (it exercises the `_unpublish_generation` compensation the new gate calls); `pytest tests/unit tests/contracts` → **1350 passed**.
- **Lint/type** — `ruff check apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py tests/integration/test_delete_fence_precedence.py` → **All checks passed**; `mypy apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py` → **Success: no issues found in 1 source file**.
- **Live `knowledge` untouched**: **2041 points** before the metric run, after it, and at the end. Every live-path test ran on a T-071 scratch collection.

RISKS: (1) The new gate reaches `_unpublish_generation`, so a deleted document's stale chunks/FTS/points are now removed at the **load** gate instead of the write gate. Nothing this run has written exists yet at that point, so in practice this only removes a *previous* attempt's leftovers for a dead generation — idempotent and version-scoped, which is the compensation T-019 already intended — but the removal now happens earlier in the job's life and that ordering change is real. (2) The fence-first check is scoped to the delete case; a superseded generation whose original was purged still discloses `INGESTION_ERROR`, unchanged. If T-036 wants the same precedence for `STALE_GENERATION`, that is a follow-up, not a silent widening here. (3) **Pre-existing, not mine**: `test_crash_recovery.py` and `test_ingestion_jobs.py` cannot run in one session — together they give 7 failures with my fix and **8 at HEAD** (the 8th being the red this ticket fixed), because each module claims the shared Redis queue via import-time `INGEST_QUEUE_PREFIX` and the crash suite drives real worker subprocesses. Anyone verifying this area must run those two suites separately or they will misattribute the failures. (4) Four `knowledge_test_crash_*` scratch collections (0–2 points each) had accumulated in Qdrant from the interfering multi-suite runs; a clean single crash-suite run leaves **0** (verified), I deleted the four stragglers, and no collection of mine remains. Live `knowledge` never involved. (5) The `INGESTION_ERROR` re-check adds one cheap tombstone read on that failure path only; it does not run on success. (6) No commit, no deploy, no restart, no compose command, no writes to live `knowledge`, no subagent by me. Product change confined to the fence/load ordering in `process_job` and one new helper — no other function in the file was touched.

Start→end `git status --short`: **24 → 26 entries**. **My delta: 2 paths + this row** — ` M apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py`, `?? tests/integration/test_delete_fence_precedence.py`. **Foreign paths untouched: 0** — the tree-discipline list (`packages/workflow_engine/adapters/langflow_adapter.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_adapter.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_fixture.py`, `tests/fixtures/workflows/`) and every `handoffs/*` file are exactly as found. Collision check at START: `apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py` and `packages/ingestion/dedup.py` were both clean, so dev2's T-073 area was untouched by me throughout.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-001 — Bounded calculator hardening (V3 plan trace A1-2)

TASK: T-V3-001 (solo, ~20 min, no subagents). Bound calculator input/ops so agents can't burn compute. Reject overlong expressions, deep nesting, huge exponents with typed errors; pure-AST evaluation — no eval/exec/import surface.

CHANGE (my delta = 2 product/test paths + this row, 0 foreign paths touched — `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched per hard out-of-scope):
- `packages/agents/tools.py` (M — allowed) — `CalculatorTool` now enforces declared bounds before any arithmetic: `MAX_EXPRESSION_CHARS=200` (bounds `ast.parse` itself), `MAX_AST_NODES=64` (one node = one operation), `MAX_AST_DEPTH=16` (bounds recursion; plus a source-bracket walk because `ast` erases redundant `(((1)))`), `MAX_EXPONENT=128` (the one super-linear operator), `MAX_RESULT_BITS=4096` (~1233 decimal digits, checked on every intermediate). New `CalculatorInputRejected(ValueError)` carrying `AgentErrorCode.TOOL_SCHEMA_INVALID` is *returned* as `{error, error_code}` rather than raised — the runtime maps any exception to `PROVIDER_ERROR` (`packages/agents/runtime.py:187`), which would misfile a refused input as a provider blip. `input_schema` now advertises `maxLength` and the bound description; whitelist stays `ast.Constant/BinOp/UnaryOp` + arithmetic ops only.
- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, **append-only: 301 insertions, 0 deletions**; baseline 27 tests → 39) — 12 new tests appended, none edited: huge-exponent typed rejection (7 cases incl. computed exponent), deep/wide typed rejection, node-cap shallow-tree, overlong typed rejection, non-arithmetic refusal, non-string typed rejection, no-dynamic-surface package-wide AST scan, declared-bounds sanity, ordinary-arithmetic still works, time-bounded hostile rejection (<250 ms, 100 KB garbage included), stable-code pin.
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: V3 plan trace **A1-2** — the calculator's input is chosen by the **model**, so its cost is attacker-controlled. Without bounds a 7-char `9**9**9` is minutes of big-integer work, a 40-deep `1+1+…` is deep recursion, and a 50 KB string is unbounded parse/memory. The fix is pure CPU-bound hardening (no services) and inherits Stream A (A0-1/A0-2/A1 accepted by rew1) without re-litigating it — see `handoff/DEV11-V3-HANDOFF.md`.

REUSED: `ast` stdlib + `operator` (already the evaluator's basis); `AgentErrorCode.TOOL_SCHEMA_INVALID` from `packages/agents/interfaces.py` (the A0-1 taxonomy — refusal branches on code, never on message text); `ToolSpec`/`ToolRegistry` contracts from the same module. No new dependency, no SDK, no config.

BUILT: `CalculatorInputRejected`, five `MAX_*` constants, `_as_number`/`_check_exponent`/`_guard_result`/`_check_tree`/`_check_source_nesting`/`_preflight` and the two-path `_eval_node` (structural pre-flight before compute, plus per-result bit-length and exponent-magnitude guards). Bounds chosen: 200 chars >> real expressions (documented examples are <20) but far below hostile; 64 nodes/16 depth >> real arithmetic (the documented `12*45+10` is 5 nodes, depth 2) but below linear nesting attacks; exponent 128 fits `2**128` (≈39 digits, the cap's own test) while rejecting the `9**9**9` bomb; 4096 bits is 100× a 64-bit int yet caps the `9**99999` path to a pre-compute refusal via `bit_length(base)*exponent`.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `6227b8d` at the end — T-V1-080 already committed by museR; nothing committed by me):
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_agents.py -q` → **67 passed** (39 total: 27 pre-existing + 12 new; all 27 pre-existing green — append-only proven by `git diff --numstat: 301 insertions, 0 deletions`). Wider gate `pytest tests/unit tests/contracts -q` → **1390 passed, 1 warning**.
- `ruff check packages/agents` + `ruff check packages/agents/tools.py tests/unit/test_agents.py` → **All checks passed**; `mypy packages/agents/tools.py` / `mypy packages/agents/` (5 files) → **Success: no issues found**.
- **Checklist 1 — huge-exponent/deep-nesting rejected typed:** `9**9**9`, `10**10**10`, `2**1000`, `(2+3)**1000` etc. → `{"error": "expression rejected: exponent …", "error_code": "tool_schema_invalid"}`; deep chains `1+…` and bracket nesting `((((1))))` both hit the depth guard; shallow wide tree (80 nodes, depth 3) hits node cap. All <1 ms.
- **Checklist 2 — no eval surface:** package-wide AST walk over `packages/agents/*.py` finds zero `eval`/`exec`/`compile`/`__import__` calls and zero `importlib`/`subprocess`/`ctypes` imports; `tools.py` additionally bans `getattr`/`setattr`/`open`/`globals`; plus a source-level `inspect.getsource(CalculatorTool._eval_node)` pin that evaluation is `ast.`-driven.
- **Checklist 3 — bounded nodes/depth/exponent magnitude:** module-level `MAX_*` plus `CalculatorTool.MAX_*` plus `input_schema.properties.expression.maxLength` all agree; every hostile input in the 23-case probe refused in <1 ms, total 7.5 ms including 100 KB garbage (refused on length before parse); two safe mutations (node cap removed → 1 failure, source-nesting check removed → 3 failures) prove the tests are discriminating — not relaxing exponent/result caps for mutations because that would detonate the bomb being bounded.
- **No service involvement** — pure CPU-bound unit work; V1's stack is irrelevant here.

RISKS: (1) Bounds are deliberately tight for safety: an expression that legitimately needs >200 chars, >64 nodes, >16 depth or exponent >128 will be refused — acceptable for an agent calculator (real arithmetic is far smaller) and preferring a refused tool call over an unbounded one. (2) `CalculatorInputRejected` is `ValueError`; a caller catching `ValueError` broadly will see it — but `execute` never raises it (it returns the typed payload), and the runtime's dispatch already maps *any* exception to `PROVIDER_ERROR`, so the return shape is the correct taxonomy boundary. (3) No workflow_engine change — `packages/workflow_engine/**` and `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched as required. (4) No commit/deploy/restart/compose.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-002 — ACL-scoped knowledge_search (V3 plan trace A1-1a)

TASK: T-V3-002 (solo, ~25 min, no subagents). Wire search as a callable tool through the registry with workspace_id enforced SERVER-SIDE (never from model args — confused deputy), strict ToolSpec schema, byte-bounded output, negative test proving cross-workspace reads rejected.

CHANGE (my delta = 3 allowed paths + this row, 0 foreign paths touched — `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched, `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched):

Defensive re-filter drops any out-of-scope items a buggy store returned, snippets truncated and payload fitted by `_snippet` + `_fit_output` until JSON ≤16384 bytes.
- `packages/agents/contract_tests.py` (M — allowed) — `check_knowledge_search_tool_contract(tool)` pins name, READ, strict schema (no workspace_id in input_schema, `additionalProperties false`, query maxLength 500, top_k 1-10) via `ToolSpec` frozen checks.
- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, append-only) — 9 new tests: contract strict/readonly/scoped, workspace-enforced server-side (fake orchestrator capture), cross-workspace reads rejected (fake store returns victim+allowed, only allowed survives), runtime BLOCKED for model-supplied `workspace_id`, strict schema rejects extra `filter/extra` fields (undeclared), output bounded (20×5000-char hits → ≤16384 bytes, snippets ≤800), overlong query/invalid top_k typed rejections, no-write-surface AST scan (no upsert/delete/eval), retrieval adapter boundary (no qdrant_client import, uses orchestrator.retrieve).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: V3 plan trace **A1-1a** — the agent's search must be usable by the model but never let the model choose whose data it sees. Server-side workspace enforcement closes the confused-deputy path (model-supplied workspace_id) that ACL-before-ranking (R-004) exists to prevent; strict schema closes smuggling of filter fields; byte-bounding closes token-budget DoS. Inherits Stream A (A0-1/A0-2/A1 accepted by rew1) without re-litigating it.

No new dependency, no SDK.

BUILT: `KnowledgeSearchTool` + 4 MAX_* constants + `_snippet`/`_fit_output`/`_resolve_orchestrator` + double workspace enforcement (dispatch RESERVED_CONTEXT_FIELDS + tool pop + ACLScope) + defensive re-filter + contract helper + 9 tests. Bounds chosen: 500 chars >> real queries (typical <100) but below 8192-token embed window; 10 hits >> context_top_k 6 but limits 3-leg fan-out; 16384 bytes ≈ 4k tokens at 4 chars/token, fits run budget and runtime 65536 wall with headroom; 800-char snippet ≈ 200 tokens, enough provenance without blowing budget. Deliberately NOT built: write path, extra_filter passthrough, or workspace_id in schema.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-003 — Scoped source-reader (V3 plan trace A1-1b)

TASK: T-V3-003 (solo, ~20 min, no subagents). Read one chunk addressed by (doc, version, chunk) with ACL re-check AT READ TIME (not just search time — a chunk quoted in one workspace must not leak via direct read; reader re-resolves caller's scope against chunk's owning workspace/version before returning bytes).

CHANGE (my delta = 2 allowed paths + this row, 0 foreign paths touched — `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched, `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched):

ACL re-check: `chunk.workspace_id == caller workspace_id` else `WORKSPACE_MISMATCH`; `Document.workspace_id == caller` and `Document.deleted_at is None` else `SOURCE_REVOKED`; `DocumentVersion.superseded_at is None` and `document_id` matches else `SOURCE_REVOKED`/`TOOL_SCHEMA_INVALID`. Read-only by construction: only `SELECT`/`get` via `ingestion.persistence` seam (`ChunkRow/Document/DocumentVersion/get_session_factory`) — zero `upsert/delete/index/put_object` surface, zero `qdrant_client` import, store behind `retrieval.interfaces`/`persistence` adapter boundary. Output byte-bounded same budget as search: `MAX_SEARCH_OUTPUT_BYTES=16384` + `MAX_SNIPPET_CHARS=800` — snippet truncated, payload `_fit_output` fitted, `truncated` flag when cut. Added `import uuid` at top.

WHY: V3 plan trace **A1-1b** — search returns evidence ids, but evidence is only safe if the **read** re-authorizes. Without a read-time ACL check, a chunk id leaked via search (or guessed) becomes a confused-deputy read that bypasses R-004. Exact triple prevents a fuzzy fallback that could cross boundaries (e.g., chunk exists in both workspaces with same index but different id). Inherits Stream A (A0-1/A0-2/A1 accepted by rew1) plus T-V3-002 search, without re-litigating it.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-004 — Typed action parsing + output bounds (V3 plan trace runtime)

TASK: T-V3-004 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). Replace regex ReAct with bounded typed-action parser. Bound parse size AND time; unknown action → BLOCKED SCHEMA_INVALID (never exec — parser produces data, never code paths); 10KB-garbage fuzz → BLOCKED with no hang; model prose never persisted as evidence.

CHANGE (my delta = 2 allowed paths + this row, 0 foreign paths touched — `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched, `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched, `re.search/match` on model output removed from touched path):
- `packages/agents/runtime.py` (M — allowed, 573→778 +205 lines, `import re` removed) — replaces `re.search(r"Action:…")` dispatch with bounded typed parser:
  - `MAX_ACTION_TEXT_CHARS=10_240` (≈10 KB) bounds the text the parser ever looks at — first check before any scan, so a 100 MB string never copied. 10_240 chars ≈10 KB, the largest model output we expect (4096 tokens≈16 KB worst-case); string ops on 10 KB is <1 ms, so 10 KB garbage fuzz is bounded by size before parse.
  - `MAX_PARSE_TIME_S=0.5` is a **real wall-clock guard**: `await asyncio.wait_for(asyncio.to_thread(_parse_action_sync, text, allowed), timeout=0.5)` — not a hoped-for fast parse. 0.5 s is 500× typical <1 ms parse but still < run's 300 s deadline, proving fuzz cannot hang the worker. Size-first check makes time guard a second wall.
  - `ParsedAction(kind="tool_call"|"final_answer"|"invalid", tool_name, raw_input, final_answer, error, error_code)` — parser produces data, never a code path. `_parse_action_sync` is linear `find` + manual brace counting (no regex, no backtracking), validates `Action:` name `[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+` and `Action Input` JSON object (balanced `{}`/`[]` scan, `json.loads` must be dict), `Final Answer:` only if no `Action:` (prose alone → invalid). Unknown tool name is **not** a parse error here — dispatch still enforces policy (keeps `test_enforced_dispatch_denies_unlisted_tool` green); parser unknown is for syntactically invalid `Action:` (empty name, missing input JSON).
  - `run_enforced` loop now: `text = gen_res.text.strip()` + `history` append, then `parsed = await _parse_action_bounded(text, allowed_tools=set(policy.allowed_tools))`. `final_answer` → `COMPLETED` with `final_output` set; `tool_call` → existing `_dispatch` (policy→reserved→schema→timeout→output) and `TOOL_OUTPUT/ERROR` step; `invalid` (garbage/unknown/prose) → `BLOCKED` `SCHEMA_INVALID` step with `tool_output={"error":…, "error_code":"schema_invalid"}`, `AgentStepType.ERROR`, `Observation` fed back, `continue` — never `tool.execute`, never `final_output = prose`, never evidence. Legacy `run` also switched from regex to `_parse_action_sync` and its final `else` prose→`FINAL_ANSWER` changed to `ERROR`+`continue` (prose never evidence in both paths).
  - Grep-prove: `grep -n "re\.search\|re\.match" packages/agents/runtime.py` → 0 (no `import re` remains for dispatch).
- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, append-only) — 6 new tests: bounded size+time (overlong → invalid, 10 KB garbage <0.5s, real action still parses), unknown→BLOCKED never exec (TrackingTool counts 0, metric unknown executed 0), fuzz 10KB garbage BLOCKED no hang (<2 s run, bounded parser <0.7 s), prose never evidence (prose-only LLM turn → BLOCKED, final answer still `the real answer`, prose not in evidence), no regex surviving (file lacks `re.search/match`), garbage wall-clock bounded (braces fuzz <0.5 s).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: V3 plan trace **runtime** — model output is untrusted input to a parser, not a program to `eval` or regex to dispatch. Unbounded `re.search` with `.*` on 10 KB+ can backtrack and hang the worker (ReDoS); even linear regex still lets a model force unbounded work and smuggle unknown tools into code paths. Typed parser closes both: size+time bounded (10 KB, 0.5 s wall-clock) with 500× headroom, and unknown→BLOCKED data (parser never calls `tool.execute`).

REUSED: `asyncio.wait_for` + `to_thread` (stdlib wall-clock, no new dep), `json.loads` for typed `Action Input` validation, `AgentErrorCode.SCHEMA_INVALID` (existing taxonomy), `ToolRegistry`/`ToolPolicy`/`RunContext` contracts, `AgentStepType.ERROR` for BLOCKED trace. No new dependency, no workflow_engine, no SDK.

BUILT: `MAX_ACTION_TEXT_CHARS`, `MAX_PARSE_TIME_S`, `ParsedAction`, `_parse_action_sync`, `_parse_action_bounded`, and the two loop rewrites (≈60 lines of dispatch replaced with typed branches). Bounds chosen: 10_240≈10 KB covers 4096-token model max with margin, string scan <1 ms, 0.5 s is 500× that but < deadline; stated in code comment and here. Deliberately NOT built: regex fallback, prose-as-final, or time hope without `wait_for`.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-005 — Retry policy + error classification (V3 plan trace runtime)

TASK: T-V3-005 (solo, ~20 min, no subagents). Retry TRANSIENTS only, per RetryPolicy. TOOL_TIMEOUT retries N then FAILED; TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED never retried; attempts persisted (visible in run record, not in-memory-only); backoff bounded by deadline (not unbounded exponential — state the cap and why).

CHANGE (my delta = 2 allowed paths + this row, 0 foreign paths touched — `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched, `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched):
- `packages/agents/runtime.py` (M — allowed, 764→885 +121 lines) — pure classifier + bounded retry loop, no new dependency:
  - **Pure classifier** `is_retryable(error_code, policy) -> bool`: `error_code in policy.retryable_error_codes` — single place that decides retryable vs fatal, shared with T-052 discipline. `TOOL_TIMEOUT` is the canonical transient (retryable when listed), `TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED` (scope/policy) is never in the default retryable set, so it never retries — misclassification is disclosure-grade, hence pure.
  - **Bounded backoff** `compute_backoff(attempt, policy) = min(initial * multiplier^(attempt-1), max_backoff_s)` and `bounded_backoff(attempt, policy, deadline) = min(compute_backoff(...), deadline - now)` — exponential but **capped** at `max_backoff_s` (default 30 s) and at wall-clock `deadline` (RunContext.deadline). Without cap, 4 attempts with 10 s/2× would be 10+20+40+80=150 s unbounded; with cap 30 s it is 10+20+30+30=90 s, and with a tight deadline it shrinks to `deadline-now`. Total wait worst-case is `(max_attempts-1)*max_backoff`, not exponential to infinity — why the cap: prevents a flapping tool holding the worker for minutes and guarantees the run never sleeps past its wall-clock deadline.
  - `spec_for` now honours per-tool `retry_policy` and `timeout_s` when the tool declares them (tests inject them), otherwise ToolSpec defaults (timeout 30 s, single attempt) — existing tools unchanged.
  - `run_enforced` tool-call branch rewritten as **retry loop**: `attempt=1..max_attempts`, each `await _dispatch(...)` result is persisted as a distinct `AgentStep` with `tool_output["attempt"]`/`"max_attempts"`/`"error_code"` — attempts visible in `run.steps`, not in-memory only. `status SUCCEEDED` or `not is_retryable` or `attempt >= max_attempts` → break; `is_retryable` and more attempts → `delay = bounded_backoff(attempt, policy, deadline)`, `await asyncio.sleep(delay)` (bounded), then `deadline`/`_check_step_budget` before next attempt. On exhaustion, `state.transition_to(FAILED, error_code, error_detail=f"{code}: tool … failed after {attempt} attempts: …")` and `agent_run.status/error` set — the run itself becomes `FAILED`, the next LLM turn does not clear it (transient exhaustion → FAILED: yes).
- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, append-only) — 5 new tests: `test_retry_classifier_is_pure_and_correct` (pure, TOOL_TIMEOUT vs TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED vs empty), `test_backoff_bounded_not_unbounded_exponential` (1,2,3 capped, total 12 vs 31, deadline caps to 0.05), `test_tool_timeout_retries_n_then_failed_attempts_persisted` (flaky_timeout sleeps 0.5s with timeout 0.05s, `max_attempts=3` → 3 ERROR steps with `attempt`/`max_attempts`/`error_code`, `run.error` contains `tool_timeout`, `elapsed <2s`), `test_tool_not_allowed_never_retried_attempts_one` (hidden_tool blocked → 1 step, `attempt==1`, `error_code tool_not_allowed`, `is_retryable` false, metric policy-error retries 0), `test_retry_backoff_bounded_by_deadline_computed_not_slept` (10,20,40,80→10,20,30,30, deadline 0.1 s caps to 0.11, no real 60 s sleep).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: V3 plan trace **runtime** — retry is a reliability seam, but retrying a policy error (scope) is a confused-deputy retry that would let a model brute-force a forbidden workspace, while not retrying a transient (timeout) would make the agent fragile. The classifier must be pure and shared with T-052's disclosure discipline; backoff must be bounded or a single flapping tool can hold the worker past its deadline. Inherits A1 tool chain + T-V3-004 parser, without re-litigating it.

REUSED: `RetryPolicy` (`max_attempts`, `initial_backoff_s`, `backoff_multiplier`, `max_backoff_s`, `retryable_error_codes`), `AgentErrorCode.TOOL_TIMEOUT/TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED/TOOL_NOT_FOUND`, `ToolSpec.retry_policy/timeout_s`, `RunContext.deadline/budget`, `AgentStep`/`AgentRun` persistence, `asyncio.sleep`/`wait_for` (stdlib). No new dependency, no workflow_engine.

BUILT: `is_retryable`, `compute_backoff`, `bounded_backoff` (all pure, 15 lines), `spec_for` per-tool honouring, and the retry loop (≈60 lines) with persisted `attempt`/`max_attempts`/`error_code` and deadline-capped sleep. Caps chosen: `max_backoff_s=30.0` (default) caps exponential at 30 s — a tool that would otherwise sleep 80 s on 4th retry sleeps 30 s; `deadline` caps to wall-clock so a run with 5 s left never sleeps 30 s. Deliberately NOT built: unbounded exponential, retry of scope errors, or in-memory-only counter.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `45561cf` at the end — T-V3-004 committed; nothing committed by me):
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_agents.py -q` → **97 passed** (92 from T-V3-004 + 5 new; all 92 pre-existing green).
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/ruff check packages/agents/runtime.py tests/unit/test_agents.py` → **All checks passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/mypy packages/agents/runtime.py tests/unit/test_agents.py` → **Success: no issues found in 2 source files** (runtime + test).
- **Checklist 1 — attempts persisted:** `flaky_timeout` with `max_attempts=3` → 3 `ERROR` steps, each `tool_output["attempt"]` 1..3 and `max_attempts` 3, `run.steps` length 3, not in-memory only.
- **Checklist 2 — no scope-error retry:** `hidden_tool` (TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED) via `run_enforced` with `allowed_tools=["allowed_tool"]` → 1 step, `attempt==1`, `error_code tool_not_allowed`, `is_retryable(TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED, policy_with_TIMEOUT) is False`, metric policy-error retries 0.
- **Checklist 3 — backoff bounded:** `compute_backoff(1..5)` with 1 s/2×/cap 3 s → 1,2,3,3,3 total 12 vs unbounded 31; `bounded_backoff` with deadline +50 ms → ≤0.06 s; `past deadline →0`; worst-case total with 10 s/2×/30 s/4 attempts → 90 vs 150 unbounded, all computed (no real 60 s sleep), elapsed for 3-timeout test <2.0 s (0.05 s timeout ×3 + 0.01+0.02 backoff).
- **Metrics:** policy-error retries: 0 (hidden_tool 1 attempt); transient exhaustion → FAILED: yes (flaky_timeout after 3 → `run.status FAILED`, `run.error` contains `tool_timeout`, `state.transition_to(FAILED)`).
- **Pure classifier:** `is_retryable` unit test proves `TOOL_TIMEOUT` true only when listed, `TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED` false with timeout-only policy, `None` false, empty policy false.

RISKS: (1) Retry loop adds up to `(max_attempts-1)` sleeps per tool call — with default `max_attempts=1` it adds zero; with `max_attempts=3` and `initial 0.01` it adds ~0.03 s, not a liveness issue. (2) Each retry is a persisted `ERROR` step and increments `tool_calls` budget — a tool that always times out with `max_attempts=5` burns 5 tool_calls, which may hit `max_tool_calls` budget and become `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` (correct, not silent). (3) Deadline cap means a retry may sleep 0 s when deadline is imminent and then immediately check `_check_step_budget` → `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` — bounded, not hung. (4) No workflow_engine change — `packages/workflow_engine/**` and `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched. (5) No commit/deploy/restart/compose.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-006 — Budget reservation before spend (V3 plan trace runtime)

TASK: T-V3-006 (solo, ~20 min, no subagents). Reserve tokens/tool-calls/deadline BEFORE each call; persist usage (survives simulated retry — usage state is durable, not reconstructed); LIMIT_EXCEEDED durable + monotonic (once exceeded, stays exceeded — no reset path that lets a run spend past the cap); zero model calls after deadline; MISSING usage data means DENY, never unlimited (no unlimited default anywhere — grep-prove no None-means-infinite).

CHANGE (my delta = 2 allowed paths + this row, 0 foreign paths touched — `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched, `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched):
- `packages/agents/runtime.py` (M — allowed, 879→1059 +180 lines) — budget as reservation, not after-the-fact check:
  - **Pure reservation helpers** `can_reserve_tokens(usage, budget, requested)`, `can_reserve_tool_call(usage, budget)`, `can_reserve_before_llm(usage, budget, deadline, estimated)` — all `if usage is None: return False` (DENY, never `True` for unlimited), `used + requested <= max`, `isinstance` check for `Usage`. No `None` path returns `True` — grep `None.*unlimited` and `if budget.max_tokens is None` have zero matches.
  - **Bounded backoff helpers** already from T-V3-005 reused (`compute_backoff`, `bounded_backoff` capped at `max_backoff_s` and `deadline`).
  - **`is_limit_exceeded(state)` + monotonic `RunState`**: `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` is terminal via `RunState.is_terminal` and `transition_to`'s one-way guard — once `state.status == LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, any `transition_to(COMPLETED)` raises `InvalidRunTransitionError`, so a cap cannot un-trip. `_PERSISTED_RUNS/_STATES/_USAGES` dicts hold `AgentRun`/`RunState`/`Usage` keyed by `run_id`; `run_enforced` at entry checks `if is_limit_exceeded(existing_state): return existing_run` (zero spend after exceeded) and re-hydrates `usage` from `existing_run.total_tokens` + `tool_calls` count — usage survives simulated retry/restart, not reconstructed from zero.
  - **`run_enforced` reservation before spend**:
    - `total_tokens = usage.prompt_tokens+completion_tokens` and `tool_calls = usage.tool_calls` seeded from persisted `usage`/`AgentRun`, not 0.
    - Before LLM: `if datetime.now(UTC) >= deadline → LIMIT_EXCEEDED` (zero calls after deadline), `estimated = model_policy.max_output_tokens`, `if not can_reserve_tokens(usage, budget, estimated) → LIMIT_EXCEEDED` before `await llm.generate`.
    - After LLM: `total_tokens += step_tokens`, `usage = Usage(prompt_tokens=total_tokens, ...)`, `agent_run.total_tokens = total_tokens`, `_PERSISTED_*` updated — durable.
    - Before tool (including each retry): `if not can_reserve_tool_call(usage, budget) → LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `if datetime.now >= deadline → LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, then retry loop with `bounded_backoff` capped and `tool_calls` persisted per attempt (`usage.tool_calls = tool_calls`, `_PERSISTED_USAGES` updated, `persisted_output["attempt"]`).
  - `spec_for` already honours per-tool `retry_policy`/`timeout_s`; no new unlimited default.
- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, append-only) — 6 new tests: `test_no_unlimited_default_grep_prove` (can_reserve on None → False, no `if budget.max_tokens is None` in file), `test_durable_limit_state_monotonic` (LIMIT_EXCEEDED → COMPLETED raises, terminal stays), `test_usage_survives_simulated_retry_and_cap_still_enforced` (small budget 10, consume 5, persisted usage intact, `can_reserve` for 6 → False, retry with same run_id still sees usage and denies), `test_zero_model_calls_after_deadline` (past deadline → 0 LLM calls, `can_reserve_before_llm` false), `test_spend_after_exceeded_refused_overspend_zero` (pre-seeded LIMIT_EXCEEDED run → immediate return, 0 new steps, overspend 0), `test_budget_reservation_before_spend_not_after` (pure: 9+2>10 deny, 9+1 allow, tool_calls 1+1 allow, 2+1 deny, None deny).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: V3 plan trace **runtime** — a cap checked *after* spend can overspend by one call, and a cap that can un-trip is not a cap. Reservation before spend makes overspend cases 0; durable usage makes a crash-then-retry that reconstructs usage from zero not bypass the cap; monotonic `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` makes the limit durable; deadline as hard wall makes zero calls after deadline; `None`→DENY makes missing data never become unlimited — the load-bearing monotonic invariant is pinned by `test_spend_after_exceeded_refused_overspend_zero` attempting spend-after-exceeded and asserting refusal.

REUSED: `RunBudget` (`max_tokens`, `max_tool_calls`, `max_steps`, `max_wall_clock_s`), `Usage` (`prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens`, `tool_calls`), `RunState`/`RunStatus.LIMIT_EXCEEDED`/`AgentRunStatus`, `AgentErrorCode.BUDGET_EXCEEDED/DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`, `RunContext.deadline/budget/model_policy`, `AgentRun.total_tokens/steps` as durable store, `is_retryable`/`compute_backoff`/`bounded_backoff` from T-V3-005. No new dependency, no workflow_engine.

BUILT: `can_reserve_tokens`, `can_reserve_tool_call`, `can_reserve_before_llm`, `is_limit_exceeded`, `_PERSISTED_RUNS/_STATES/_USAGES` (3 dicts), reservation checks before LLM (deadline, token estimate) and before tool (tool_calls, deadline), persisted `usage`/`total_tokens`/`tool_calls` after each spend, and 6 tests. Caps stated: `max_backoff_s=30.0` default caps exponential at 30 s (10,20,40→30), `deadline` caps to wall-clock so total wait never pushes past deadline — worst-case total `(max_attempts-1)*max_backoff` bounded, not exponential to infinity.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `9e7227c` at the end — T-V3-005 committed; nothing committed by me):
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_agents.py -q` → **103 passed** (97 from T-V3-005 + 6 new; all 97 pre-existing green).
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/ruff check packages/agents/runtime.py tests/unit/test_agents.py` → **All checks passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/mypy packages/agents/runtime.py tests/unit/test_agents.py` → **Success: no issues found in 2 source files** (runtime + test).
- `grep -rn "None-means-unlimited\|if budget.max_tokens is None\|if usage is None.*return True" packages/agents/runtime.py` → **0** (no None-means-infinite).
- **Checklist 1 — no unlimited default:** `can_reserve_tokens(None, budget, 1) is False`, `can_reserve_tool_call(None, budget) is False`, file grep 0.
- **Checklist 2 — durable limit state:** `RunState(LIMIT_EXCEEDED)` → `transition_to(COMPLETED)` raises `InvalidRunTransitionError`, `is_limit_exceeded` true, terminal stays.
- **Checklist 3 — usage survives retry:** small budget 10, consume 5 via LLM, `_PERSISTED_USAGES[run_id]` still 5, `can_reserve_tokens(persisted, budget, 6) is False`, second `run_enforced` with same `run_id` sees same usage and still denies (not reset to 0).
- **Zero calls after deadline:** past `deadline` → `0` LLM `generate` calls, `run.status` LIMIT_EXCEEDED, `can_reserve_before_llm` false.
- **Monotonic + overspend 0:** pre-seeded `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` run with same `run_id` → immediate return, `len(run.steps)==0` new spend, overspend cases 0.
- **Reservation before spend:** `9+2>10` deny, `9+1` allow, `tool_calls 2+1` deny, `None` deny.

RISKS: (1) Reservation uses `model_policy.max_output_tokens` as estimate — a model that actually uses fewer tokens will have reserved more than needed, but never overspends; true usage is reconciled after the call. (2) Persisted store is in-memory (`_PERSISTED_*` dicts) — survives simulated retry within same process (as the test does), but a real process restart would need DB persistence (future, not in this ticket). (3) Tool retry loop also respects budget/deadline per attempt — a flapping timeout with 5 attempts that hits `max_tool_calls` mid-retry becomes `LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, correctly not unlimited. (4) No workflow_engine change — `packages/workflow_engine/**` and `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched. (5) No commit/deploy/restart/compose.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-007 — Cancellation + terminal-state monotonicity (V3 plan trace runtime)

TASK: T-V3-007 (solo, ~20 min, no subagents). Cancel mid-tool STOPS the loop (no further dispatches after cancel — post-cancel dispatches: 0); terminal transitions ONE-WAY (COMPLETED/CANCELLED/FAILED never leave terminal state — same monotonicity discipline as T-006's cap); cancel-after-COMPLETED is a clean no-op (not an error, not a state change).

CHANGE (my delta = 3 allowed paths + this row, 0 foreign paths touched — `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched, `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched, `re.search/match` on model output already 0 from T-V3-004):
- `packages/agents/interfaces.py` (M — allowed, +24 lines) — `RunState.request_cancel() -> bool` and `is_cancelled() -> bool`: `request_cancel` checks `is_terminal(status)` first — if already `COMPLETED`/`CANCELLED`/`FAILED`/`LIMIT_EXCEEDED`/`PARTIAL` it returns `False` and changes nothing (byte-identical, no error), otherwise `transition_to(CANCELLED, CANCELLED, "run cancelled")` and returns `True`. Monotonicity is via existing `transition_to`'s `if is_terminal(self.status): raise InvalidRunTransitionError` — a cap that can un-trip is not a cap, same discipline as T-V3-006's `LIMIT_EXCEEDED`. `is_cancelled()` is `status == CANCELLED`.
- `packages/agents/runtime.py` (M — allowed, +81 lines) — `AgentRuntime.cancel_run(state, run) -> bool` terminal-aware wrapper (checks `is_terminal`, calls `state.request_cancel()`, updates `run.status = _legacy_status(CANCELLED)=FAILED`, `run.error`, `finished_at`, persists to `_PERSISTED_*`, returns `True`/`False`), plus `run_enforced` cancellation wins:
  - Top of loop `if state.is_cancelled(): agent_run.status = _legacy_status(CANCELLED); break` — no new spend, no dispatches after cancel.
  - Before each tool attempt `if state.is_cancelled(): break` and `if not can_reserve_tool_call(...)` etc., already bounds, now also cancel-aware.
  - Tool's `execute` can call `state.request_cancel()` mid-tool (test's `_CancelTool` does), the next loop iteration sees `is_cancelled()` and stops — dispatch spy shows 0 post-cancel calls.
  - `except asyncio.CancelledError` around `await llm.generate` and `await asyncio.sleep` for backoff — if the outer `run_enforced` task is `cancel()`-ed, it transitions to `CANCELLED` (if not already terminal) and breaks, never dispatching further tools. `CancelledError` is not treated as `PROVIDER_ERROR`.
  - `is_limit_exceeded` already monotonic; `cancel` uses same `transition_to` so `CANCELLED` also monotonic.
- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, append-only) — 4 new tests: `test_mid_tool_cancel_stops_loop_no_further_dispatches` (pre-seeded `RunState` in `_PERSISTED_STATES`, `_CancelTool` calls `state.request_cancel()` on first `execute`, LLM wants 3 tool calls, assert `len(calls)==1` post-cancel dispatches 0, `state.status==CANCELLED`, `run.status==FAILED` via `_legacy_status`), `test_terminal_transitions_one_way_exhaust_matrix` (loops every `terminal in _TERMINAL_STATUSES` × every `target in RunStatus` — 5×9=45 transitions — all must raise `InvalidRunTransitionError`, state byte-identical, plus active→COMPLETED then terminal rejects all), `test_cancel_after_completed_is_clean_noop_byte_identical` (state COMPLETED + run COMPLETED, `before_state=model_dump_json()`, `request_cancel` returns False, `model_dump_json()` identical, also `cancel_run` on `run2` identical), `test_cancel_wins_over_budget_and_is_monotonic` (RUNNING→CANCELLED, then every `COMPLETED/FAILED/RUNNING/LIMIT_EXCEEDED` rejected, second cancel returns False).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: V3 plan trace **runtime** — a cancel that doesn't stop dispatch is a runaway-compute bug, same seriousness as the budget cap. Mid-tool cancel must win over the next dispatch, otherwise a user-cancelled run keeps burning tokens/tools. Terminal one-way is the same monotonicity as T-006's `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` — a `COMPLETED` that can become `CANCELLED` (or vice versa) lets a run be re-opened after it should be done, violating the durable record. Cancel-after-completed must be no-op, not an error, so a late cancel from a UI race doesn't corrupt a completed run's record (byte-identical).

REUSED: `RunState`/`RunStatus`/`AgentErrorCode.CANCELLED`/`InvalidRunTransitionError` (existing monotonic terminal), `AgentRun`/`AgentStep`/`_legacy_status` (LEGACY mapping `CANCELLED→FAILED`), `_PERSISTED_RUNS/_STATES/_USAGES` (durable store from T-V3-006), `asyncio.CancelledError`/`wait_for`/`to_thread` (existing wall-clock), `ToolRegistry`/`_ScriptedLLM` test harness. No new dependency, no workflow_engine.

BUILT: `request_cancel`/`is_cancelled` (15 lines), `cancel_run` (20 lines), 3 cancellation checks in `run_enforced` (top, before tool, `CancelledError` handlers for LLM and backoff), and 4 tests. Deliberately NOT built: a separate cancel token or `asyncio.Event` — `RunState` itself is the token, persisted and polled, so a crash-then-retry that reloads `RunState` still sees `CANCELLED` without a new primitive.

- **Checklist 1 — mid-tool cancel stops loop:** `_CancelTool` with `state.request_cancel()` on first `execute`, LLM wants 3 tool calls, `len(calls)==1`, `post-cancel dispatches` = `len(calls)-1 ==0`, `state.status==CANCELLED`, `run.status==FAILED` (legacy mapping), no `TOOL_OUTPUT` for `cancel_tool` after cancel.
- **Checklist 2 — one-way terminals:** exhaustive 5 terminals × 9 targets =45 `transition_to` must raise `InvalidRunTransitionError`, state unchanged, plus `RUNNING→COMPLETED` then all 9 rejected — not a sample, the matrix.
- **Checklist 3 — late cancel = no-op:** `RunState(COMPLETED)` + `AgentRun(COMPLETED)` → `before_state=model_dump_json()`, `request_cancel()` returns `False`, `model_dump_json()` identical, `cancel_run` on second pair also `False` and byte-identical, `run.model_dump_json()` identical.
- **Metrics:** post-cancel dispatches: 0 (dispatch spy `len(calls)==1` for 3 requested); terminal monotonic: 45/45 rejected; late cancel byte-identical: true.
- **Cancel wins over budget:** `RUNNING→CANCELLED` then every `COMPLETED/FAILED/RUNNING/LIMIT_EXCEEDED` rejected, second `request_cancel` returns `False`.

RISKS: (1) `cancel_run` updates `run.finished_at` on success — a cancel that races with a completing `run_enforced` that already set `finished_at` will overwrite it with a slightly later timestamp, but `state` and `error` remain `CANCELLED` (the run's terminal time is not part of the monotonic guarantee). (2) `state.request_cancel()` is synchronous — a tool that calls it mid-`execute` will not abort the current `tool.execute`'s `await` (the tool already started), but the next dispatch is blocked, which is exactly "no further dispatches". (3) `asyncio.CancelledError` from outer task cancellation is caught and mapped to `CANCELLED` only if not already terminal — a `CANCELLED` run that is then `CancelledError`-ed remains `CANCELLED` (second cancel is no-op, not error). (4) No workflow_engine change — `packages/workflow_engine/**` and `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched. (5) No commit/deploy/restart/compose.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-008 — Approval / waiting_input gate (V3 plan trace V3 plan)

TASK: T-V3-008 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). Unapproved WRITE → BLOCKED/WAITING_APPROVAL (never executes); approved run resumes; approval scoped to (run + principal) — an approval for run A by principal P authorizes nothing for run B or principal Q; resume re-checks ACL AND policy revision (a policy change or revocation between approval and resume must re-block, not ride the stale approval); READS need no approval (T-002 search + T-003 reader stay approval-free — prove it, don't break it).

CHANGE (my delta = 2 allowed paths + this row, 0 foreign paths touched — `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched, `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched, `re.search/match` on model output already 0 from T-V3-004):
- `packages/agents/runtime.py` (M — allowed, 1059→1395 +336 lines) — approval gate for WRITE, scoped and re-checked:
  - `_APPROVALS: dict[(run_id, principal_id, tool_name), policy_revision]` and `_WAITING: dict[run_id, (tool_name, raw_input, policy_revision, principal_id)]` — in-memory durable store, scoped to (run, principal, tool) and revision. `approve_run(run_id, principal_id, tool_name, policy_revision)` records `policy_revision`; `_is_approved` checks `dict.get(key) == policy_revision`; `_is_write_tool` via `_tool_class_for`.
  - `run_enforced` before WRITE dispatch: `if _is_write_tool(tool_name) and not _is_approved(run_id, principal_id, tool_name, authz_policy_revision)` → store `_WAITING[run_id]`, `state.transition_to(WAITING_APPROVAL, TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED)`, `agent_run.steps.append(ERROR)`, `history` observation, `break` — never `tool.execute`, metric unapproved writes 0. `is_write_tool` ensures `knowledge_search`/`knowledge_read`/`calculator` (READ) bypass the gate (existing search/reader tests still green).
  - Resume re-check at entry: if `state.status == WAITING_APPROVAL`, load `waiting = _WAITING[run_id]`, check `_is_approved(run_id, principal_id, w_tool, authz_policy_revision)` and `w_principal == principal_id` — cross-run (different `run_id`) and cross-principal (different `principal_id`) both fail, and a stale `policy_revision` (approve at rev1, resume with rev2) also fails (check-then-act across time, same bug class as T-050). On fail, stay `WAITING_APPROVAL` with `ERROR` step and `return` — zero dispatches. On pass, clear `_WAITING`, transition `WAITING_APPROVAL → RUNNING` (active, not terminal, so allowed), then dispatch the waiting tool directly (without new LLM call) with full retry/budget/deadline/cancel handling, including `can_reserve_tool_call` and `bounded_backoff` caps.
  - `cancel` already from T-V3-007 (`request_cancel` monotonic) — approval gate respects it: `WAITING_APPROVAL` is active, not terminal, so cancel can still win.
- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, append-only) — 5 new tests: `test_reads_approval_free_search_and_reader_stay_green` (READ search+reader without approval succeed, `_is_write_tool` false for search/reader/calculator, true for publish), `test_approval_scoped_cross_run_and_cross_principal_both_fail` (direct `_is_approved` for run-A/P vs run-B/P and run-A/Q, plus `run_enforced` integration for cross-run and cross-principal via `WAITING_APPROVAL` steps), `test_unapproved_write_never_executes` (spy WRITE `publish` without approval → 0 calls, `ERROR` step), `test_approved_write_resumes_and_succeeds` (first run WAITING, `approve_run`, resume with same run/principal/rev → 1 call, `TOOL_OUTPUT`), `test_resume_rechecks_acl_and_policy_revision_stale_approval_reblocks` (approve rev1, resume with rev2 → still WAITING 0 calls, then approve rev2 → 1 call). All 5 prove both replay directions and stale-ride-through.
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: V3 plan trace **V3 plan** — a scoped approval that can be replayed across runs or principals is theater, not control; a stale approval that rides through a revocation is the same race as T-050 (check at approval time, act at resume time, policy changed in between). The gate must be (run, principal, tool, revision) scoped and re-checked at resume, and READS must stay free so the T-002/T-003 chain isn't broken. Inherits T-002/T-007 without re-litigating.

REUSED: `ToolClass.WRITE` via `_tool_class_for` (existing write set `publish` etc.), `RunStatus.WAITING_APPROVAL` (active, not terminal, already in `_ACTIVE_STATUSES`), `AgentErrorCode.TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED` (existing taxonomy for unapproved), `RunContext.run_id/principal_id/authz_policy_revision/allowed_tools` (server-side, not model), `ToolRegistry`/`_dispatch` (existing strict dispatch), `_PERSISTED_*` durable store from T-V3-006, `is_retryable`/`bounded_backoff` caps. No new dependency, no workflow_engine.

BUILT: `_APPROVALS`/`_WAITING` (2 dicts), `_is_approved`/`_is_write_tool`/`approve_run` (3 pure/helpers), `run_enforced` approval gate (store waiting + transition to `WAITING_APPROVAL` + `BREAK`) and resume re-check (cross-run/principal/policy + direct dispatch of waiting tool with full retry/budget handling), and 5 tests. Scoped to (run, principal, tool, revision) with re-check at resume — replay across runs/principals fails, stale rev2 re-blocks. READS bypass the gate (`_is_write_tool` false).

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-009 — Step-event persistence adapter (V3 plan trace V3 plan)

TASK: T-V3-009 (solo, ~25 min, no subagents). NEW packages/agents/step_store.py — append/list StepEvent store with MONOTONIC sequence enforcement. Out-of-order append REJECTED (sequence violations accepted: 0); secrets REDACTED at the boundary (never stored — deny-list + pattern redaction, state what you redact); payloads BOUNDED (state the cap); NO hidden reasoning stored (model's chain-of-thought never persists — only typed events; prove by construction: the event schema has no free-text-reasoning field).

CHANGE (my delta = 2 allowed files + this row, 0 foreign paths touched — `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched, `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched):
- `packages/agents/step_store.py` (NEW — allowed, 247 lines, ruff + mypy clean) — `StepStore` ABC (`append`/`list`/`get`) with `InMemoryStepStore` (thread-safe dict, `max_seq` per run) and `SqliteStepStore` (file `step_events` table `PRIMARY KEY (run_id, sequence)` + index). `SequenceViolationError` on `sequence != max_seq+1` (monotonic, 0 violations accepted). `_prepare_event` redacts at boundary via `_redact_text` (deny-list: `api_key`, `apikey`, `password`, `passwd`, `secret`, `token`, `bearer`, `authorization`, `sk-`, `ghp_`, `gho_`, `-----BEGIN` + patterns: `Bearer …`, `sk-…{20}`, `ghp_…{20}`, `-----BEGIN…`) and bounds: `MAX_SUMMARY_CHARS=1_024`, `MAX_REF_CHARS=4_096`, `MAX_EVENT_BYTES=32_768` — field truncation then JSON size check. No `update`/`delete`/`put`/`set` surface — append-only proven by `hasattr` check and class `__dict__` inspection. `StepEvent` schema has no `reasoning`/`chain_of_thought`/`thought` field — proven by `StepEvent.model_fields` inspection, only `action_summary` (typed, bounded) and `tool_*_ref` (references).
- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, append-only) — 7 new tests: `test_step_store_monotonic_enforces_order_inmemory` (+ `sqlite` variant) via shared `_run_step_store_suite` (append 1,2 then out-of-order 1 and skip 4 both rejected, list ordered), `test_step_store_secrets_redacted_at_boundary` (7 secrets: `api_key:`, `password=`, `Bearer sk-…`, `sk-…`, `ghp_…`, `Authorization: Bearer`, `-----BEGIN…` — each stored `[REDACTED]` and raw not in `action_summary`), `test_step_store_payloads_bounded` (5000-char summary → ≤1024, 10K refs → ≤4096, JSON ≤32768), `test_step_store_no_hidden_reasoning_by_construction` (fields check), `test_step_store_append_only_no_mutation_api` (`hasattr`/`dir` checks for no update/delete), `test_step_store_adapter_injectable_both_backends_same_interface` (same 1,2,3 sequence via both backends, callable checks).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: V3 plan trace **V3 plan** — step log must be ordered, tamper-evident, and free of secrets/hidden reasoning. Monotonic `sequence` prevents a worker from rewriting history out of order; redaction at the boundary (deny-list + pattern) ensures secrets never touch storage; 32 KB/1K/4K bounds keep a 100-step run under ~3 MB and fit SQLite pages; no `reasoning` field proves the model's chain-of-thought never persists (only typed `action_summary` + refs). Adapter shape lets tests run in-memory and prod run on SQLite file with same interface.

REUSED: `StepEvent`/`StepStatus`/`Usage` (existing V3 contracts, frozen), `sqlite3` (stdlib, no new dep), `threading.Lock` (stdlib), `json` + `re` (stdlib, for redaction patterns), `pathlib.Path` (stdlib). No workflow_engine, no new SDK, no `qdrant_client` in this file.

BUILT: `MAX_EVENT_BYTES=32_768`, `MAX_SUMMARY_CHARS=1_024`, `MAX_REF_CHARS=4_096`, `_DENY_KEYS` (13) + `_RE_SECRET_PATTERNS` (4), `_redact_text`/`_bound_text`/`_prepare_event`, `SequenceViolationError`, `StepStore` ABC, `InMemoryStepStore` and `SqliteStepStore` (same interface, `append` enforces `max_seq+1`, `list` ordered), and 7 tests. Caps chosen: 32 KB per event ×100 steps ≈3 MB, 1K summary enough for typed `tool:…` but not CoT dump, 4K refs enough for truncated JSON.

RISKS: (1) Redaction is heuristic — deny-list + 4 patterns; a novel secret format not in the list could slip (e.g. `aws_access_key` not in deny-list) — future should add to `_DENY_KEYS` as needed. (2) 32 KB cap truncates `action_summary` first, then refs — a 100-step run with many large refs will still fit, but the oldest summary may be cut to ~100 chars in worst case (acceptable for a step log). (3) `InMemoryStepStore` is process-local — a real crash loses it; `SqliteStepStore` persists to file, but both share the same monotonic check (`SELECT MAX(sequence)`), so a crash-then-retry that re-creates the store from the same file will still enforce order. (4) No workflow_engine change — `packages/workflow_engine/**` and `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched. (5) No commit/deploy/restart/compose.
